> This page presents evidence for the **prophethood** of **Prophet Muhammad** ﷺ, drawn from his recorded life. It complements the case made from [the Qur'an itself](/quran). Not every point is a decisive proof by itself. Taken together, however, they form a cumulative case for the authenticity of his mission. -- *note: this page is a work in progress*

## Overview

Everything on the [Qur'an page](/quran) was recited by **one man** who could **neither read nor write**, over twenty-three years in seventh-century Arabia. The Qur'an cannot be separated from the life of the man who delivered it, and that life is documented in unusual detail by his followers, family, servants, and enemies.

## Muhammad ﷺ: His Teachings, Character, Life, and the Case for His Prophethood

Many people first encounter Prophet Muhammad ﷺ as a name, a historical founder, or the subject of a debate. The Islamic sources preserve a much fuller picture. They describe a man who worshipped through the night and helped at home; who led a state yet died with his armor held as security for food; who wept over his child, played with his family, forgave personal injuries, restrained soldiers, protected treaty partners, listened to women and children, and repeatedly directed attention away from himself and toward God.[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[37](#ref-37){: .footnote}

This article has two related but distinct aims: to examine **what Prophet Muhammad ﷺ actually taught and did**, and to consider **why his claim to prophethood deserves to be taken seriously**. A beautiful teaching does not by itself prove revelation, and a report of a miracle does not authenticate itself. Section IV therefore considers the case for prophethood cumulatively while distinguishing between different kinds of evidence.

At the center of that claim is the Qur’an, which Prophet Muhammad ﷺ recited publicly in portions over roughly twenty-three years. It describes him as unlettered, and his first audience had already watched him live an ordinary adult life among them. Whatever conclusion one reaches, **the text and the life have to be considered together**.[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}[37](#ref-37){: .footnote}[40](#ref-40){: .footnote}[41](#ref-41){: .footnote}

For someone claiming prophethood, **his life is itself relevant evidence**. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ had lived openly among his first audience for decades before revelation. They then watched him through years of marginalization and persecution. His wives and household saw how he behaved in private, and later the same man acquired political and military power. Any explanation of his claim has to make sense of this entire course of life. The man known for truthfulness before the claim did not become a habitual liar after it. The teacher of mercy while not having authority continued to restrain retaliation when he had it. The leader who demanded justice refused special treatment for his own family, while the head of state continued to serve at home and died without accumulating a private fortune.[8](#ref-8){: .footnote}[28](#ref-28){: .footnote}[37](#ref-37){: .footnote}[38](#ref-38){: .footnote}[41](#ref-41){: .footnote}[42](#ref-42){: .footnote}

The argument on this page is **cumulative**. No single episode below is presented as a decisive proof by itself. Each shows how one man acted when he had every opportunity to act otherwise, and the pattern remains consistent from his years of powerlessness in Mecca to his rule over Arabia. The question is: what explanation accounts for **all of them together**?

### A short note on sources

- **The Qur’an** is the primary text Prophet Muhammad ﷺ publicly recited as revelation.[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
- **Hadith** are transmitted reports of his words, actions, and approvals. This article gives priority to *Sahih al-Bukhari* and *Sahih Muslim*, the two most highly regarded Sunni hadith collections. In hadith terminology, *sahih* [sound/authentic] is the standard term for a report judged to meet the highest ordinary grade of authenticity. Other collections are identified explicitly when used.[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[22](#ref-22){: .footnote}[23](#ref-23){: .footnote}[33](#ref-33){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}[44](#ref-44){: .footnote}
- **Early biography, often called *sira* [prophetic biography],** preserves important narrative material that is not always found in the major hadith collections. Its reports vary in the strength of their transmission, so biographical material is identified as such rather than treated as equivalent to a rigorously authenticated hadith.[8](#ref-8){: .footnote}[19](#ref-19){: .footnote}[28](#ref-28){: .footnote}[29](#ref-29){: .footnote}[37](#ref-37){: .footnote}[38](#ref-38){: .footnote}[39](#ref-39){: .footnote}
- Quotations from the Qur’an below are **verbatim**, drawn from Dr. Mustafa Khattab's *The Clear Quran* or Saheeh International, whichever reads more clearly in English for the point being made; each is attributed to its translator. Hadith quotations are cited by collection and report number.
- The symbol **ﷺ** means “may God’s peace and blessings be upon him and his .”

---

## I. The moral and spiritual vision he taught

Prophet Muhammad’s ﷺ teachings do not separate worship from ordinary conduct. Faith in God repeatedly appears beside duties to family, neighbors, the poor, strangers, workers, opponents, animals, and the wider community. The details below show what that moral vision looked like in practice.[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}

### A. The poor, the vulnerable, neighbors, servants, and social dignity

1. **Care for the vulnerable is part of righteousness.** The Qur’an does not reduce goodness to ritual. It joins faith to the willingness to give cherished wealth to relatives, orphans, the poor, stranded travelers, those who ask for help, and people seeking freedom from bondage.[56](#ref-56){: .footnote}[61](#ref-61){: .footnote}

   > "Righteousness is not in turning your faces towards the east or the west. Rather, the righteous are those who believe in Allah, the Last Day, the angels, the Books, and the prophets; who give charity out of their cherished wealth to relatives, orphans, the poor, ˹needy˺ travellers, beggars, and for freeing captives; who establish prayer, pay alms-tax, and keep the pledges they make; and who are patient in times of suffering, adversity, and in ˹the heat of˺ battle. It is they who are true ˹in faith˺, and it is they who are mindful ˹of Allah˺." (**Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:177**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿۞ لَّيْسَ ٱلْبِرَّ أَن تُوَلُّوا۟ وُجُوهَكُمْ قِبَلَ ٱلْمَشْرِقِ وَٱلْمَغْرِبِ وَلَـٰكِنَّ ٱلْبِرَّ مَنْ ءَامَنَ بِٱللَّهِ وَٱلْيَوْمِ ٱلْـَٔاخِرِ وَٱلْمَلَـٰٓئِكَةِ وَٱلْكِتَـٰبِ وَٱلنَّبِيِّـۧنَ وَءَاتَى ٱلْمَالَ عَلَىٰ حُبِّهِۦ ذَوِى ٱلْقُرْبَىٰ وَٱلْيَتَـٰمَىٰ وَٱلْمَسَـٰكِينَ وَٱبْنَ ٱلسَّبِيلِ وَٱلسَّآئِلِينَ وَفِى ٱلرِّقَابِ وَأَقَامَ ٱلصَّلَوٰةَ وَءَاتَى ٱلزَّكَوٰةَ وَٱلْمُوفُونَ بِعَهْدِهِمْ إِذَا عَـٰهَدُوا۟ ۖ وَٱلصَّـٰبِرِينَ فِى ٱلْبَأْسَآءِ وَٱلضَّرَّآءِ وَحِينَ ٱلْبَأْسِ ۗ أُو۟لَـٰٓئِكَ ٱلَّذِينَ صَدَقُوا۟ ۖ وَأُو۟لَـٰٓئِكَ هُمُ ٱلْمُتَّقُونَ ۝١٧٧﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ البَقَرَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Indifference to orphans and the hungry contradicts religion itself.**

   > "Have you seen the one who denies the ˹final˺ Judgment? That is the one who repulses the orphan and does not encourage the feeding of the poor." (**Sūrat al-Māʿūn 107:1-3**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿ أَرَءَيْتَ ٱلَّذِى يُكَذِّبُ بِٱلدِّينِ ۝١ فَذَٰلِكَ ٱلَّذِى يَدُعُّ ٱلْيَتِيمَ ۝٢ وَلَا يَحُضُّ عَلَىٰ طَعَامِ ٱلْمِسْكِينِ ۝٣﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ المَاعُونِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Freeing an enslaved person and feeding people in a time of hunger are among the difficult forms of goodness the Qur’an praises.**[54](#ref-54){: .footnote}[56](#ref-56){: .footnote}

   > "And what will make you realize what ˹attempting˺ the challenging path is? It is to free a slave, or to give food in times of famine, to an orphaned relative, or to a poor person in distress." (**Sūrat al-Balad 90:12-16**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَمَآ أَدْرَىٰكَ مَا ٱلْعَقَبَةُ ۝١٢ فَكُّ رَقَبَةٍ ۝١٣ أَوْ إِطْعَـٰمٌ فِى يَوْمٍ ذِى مَسْغَبَةٍ ۝١٤ يَتِيمًا ذَا مَقْرَبَةٍ ۝١٥ أَوْ مِسْكِينًا ذَا مَتْرَبَةٍ ۝١٦﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ البَلَدِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Caring for widows and the poor was given extraordinary spiritual value.**

   > "The one who works for the widow and the poor is like one striving in God's cause, or like one who prays through the night and fasts through the day." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 5353[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Caring for an orphan was described as a path to being near him in Paradise.**

   > "I and the caretaker of an orphan will be in Paradise like this," and he brought his index and middle fingers together. — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 6005[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Relieving another person’s hardship carries spiritual weight.** The same teaching joins practical acts to divine reward: lifting distress, easing a debtor’s difficulty, and protecting another person’s dignity.

   > "Whoever relieves a believer of a worldly hardship, God will relieve him of a hardship on the Day of Resurrection." — *Sahih Muslim* 2699a[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **A debtor in genuine hardship must be given time.** Better still, the Qur’an encourages the creditor to forgive some or all of the debt as charity.

   > "If it is difficult for someone to repay a debt, postpone it until a time of ease. And if you waive it as an act of charity, it will be better for you, if only you knew." (**Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:280**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَإِن كَانَ ذُو عُسْرَةٍ فَنَظِرَةٌ إِلَىٰ مَيْسَرَةٍ ۚ وَأَن تَصَدَّقُوا۟ خَيْرٌ لَّكُمْ ۖ إِن كُنتُمْ تَعْلَمُونَ ۝٢٨٠﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ البَقَرَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **A neighbor’s safety was made a matter of faith.**

   > "Whoever believes in God and the Last Day must not harm his neighbor." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 6018[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **The duty toward neighbors was emphasized with unusual force.** Gabriel spoke about it so often that Prophet Muhammad ﷺ thought neighbors might eventually be given inheritance rights.

   > "Gabriel kept instructing me concerning the neighbor until I thought he would make the neighbor an heir." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 6014[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Hospitality was a religious duty, not merely a social courtesy.** In one short teaching, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ placed honoring guests beside protecting neighbors and disciplining one’s speech.

   > "Whoever believes in God and the Last Day should honor his guest." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 6018[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Kindness was not to be reserved for one’s own circle.** He encouraged people to feed others and greet both those they knew and those they did not.

   > A man asked, "What part of Islam is best?" He said: "Feed people, and greet those you know and those you do not know." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 12[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Believers were to feel one another’s suffering as members of a single body.**

   > "The believers in their mutual affection, mercy, and compassion are like one body: when one limb suffers, the whole body responds with sleeplessness and fever." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 6011[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **He confronted ethnic contempt even within his own community.** When Abu Dharr insulted another man through his mother, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ did not dismiss it as ordinary speech. He told Abu Dharr that this attitude was a remnant of the pre-Islamic age of ignorance.

   > "You are a man in whom there remains ignorance." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 30[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **People under one’s authority retained basic human dignity.** In the same rebuke to Abu Dharr, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ required masters to give enslaved people the same standard of food and clothing they gave themselves and prohibited overwhelming them with work.

   > "Your servants are your brothers whom God placed under your authority. Feed them from what you eat and clothe them from what you wear." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 30[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **If the work given to a servant was too heavy, the master was required to help.**

   > "Do not burden them with what overwhelms them; and if you burden them, then help them." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 30[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Emancipation was made both a major good deed and a form of atonement.** The Qur’an repeatedly prescribes freeing enslaved people as a way to repair serious wrongs, while Prophet Muhammad ﷺ explicitly praised setting people free.

   > "Whoever frees a Muslim slave, God will save from the Fire a limb of his for every limb he freed." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 2517[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Human beings share one origin, and inherited racial or tribal status does not determine worth before God.**

   > "O humanity! Indeed, We created you from a male and a female, and made you into peoples and tribes so that you may ˹get to˺ know one another. Surely the most noble of you in the sight of Allah is the most righteous among you. Allah is truly All-Knowing, All-Aware." (**Sūrat al-Ḥujurāt 49:13**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿يَـٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلنَّاسُ إِنَّا خَلَقْنَـٰكُم مِّن ذَكَرٍ وَأُنثَىٰ وَجَعَلْنَـٰكُمْ شُعُوبًا وَقَبَآئِلَ لِتَعَارَفُوٓا۟ ۚ إِنَّ أَكْرَمَكُمْ عِندَ ٱللَّهِ أَتْقَىٰكُمْ ۚ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ عَلِيمٌ خَبِيرٌ ۝١٣﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الحُجُرَاتِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Human dignity comes before communal divisions.** The Qur’an describes God as having honored the children of Adam as such.

   > "Indeed, We have dignified the children of Adam, carried them on land and sea, granted them good and lawful provisions, and privileged them far above many of Our creatures." (**Sūrat al-Isrāʾ 17:70**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿۞ وَلَقَدْ كَرَّمْنَا بَنِىٓ ءَادَمَ وَحَمَلْنَـٰهُمْ فِى ٱلْبَرِّ وَٱلْبَحْرِ وَرَزَقْنَـٰهُم مِّنَ ٱلطَّيِّبَـٰتِ وَفَضَّلْنَـٰهُمْ عَلَىٰ كَثِيرٍ مِّمَّنْ خَلَقْنَا تَفْضِيلًا ۝٧٠﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الإِسۡرَاءِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Generosity was placed within everyone’s reach, including the poor.** Half a date, a good word, removing an obstacle, or helping another person can all carry moral value. Charity was not made the privilege of people with money.

   > "Do not belittle any good deed, even meeting your brother with a cheerful face." — *Sahih Muslim* 2626[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Giving in charity does not ultimately impoverish the giver.**

   > "Charity does not diminish wealth." — *Sahih Muslim* 2588[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **The preservation of human life is given immense moral weight.** In recounting a command given to the Children of Israel, the Qur’an compares the unjust killing of one person to killing all humanity, and saving one life to saving all humanity. The verse has a specific historical context, but its valuation of human life is unmistakable.

   > "That is why We ordained for the Children of Israel that whoever takes a life—unless as a punishment for murder or mischief in the land—it will be as if they killed all of humanity; and whoever saves a life, it will be as if they saved all of humanity. ˹Although˺ Our messengers already came to them with clear proofs, many of them still transgressed afterwards through the land." (**Sūrat al-Māʾidah 5:32**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿مِنْ أَجْلِ ذَٰلِكَ كَتَبْنَا عَلَىٰ بَنِىٓ إِسْرَٰٓءِيلَ أَنَّهُۥ مَن قَتَلَ نَفْسًۢا بِغَيْرِ نَفْسٍ أَوْ فَسَادٍ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ فَكَأَنَّمَا قَتَلَ ٱلنَّاسَ جَمِيعًا وَمَنْ أَحْيَاهَا فَكَأَنَّمَآ أَحْيَا ٱلنَّاسَ جَمِيعًا ۚ وَلَقَدْ جَآءَتْهُمْ رُسُلُنَا بِٱلْبَيِّنَـٰتِ ثُمَّ إِنَّ كَثِيرًا مِّنْهُم بَعْدَ ذَٰلِكَ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ لَمُسْرِفُونَ ۝٣٢﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ المَائـِدَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Appearance and wealth are not what God ultimately evaluates.** Human worth is therefore not grounded in beauty, class display, or inherited social position, but in the heart and in conduct.

   > "God does not look at your faces and your wealth, but He looks at your hearts and your deeds." — *Sahih Muslim* 2564c[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **He entrusted leadership to people whose social background others looked down on.** Zayd ibn Haritha had formerly been enslaved and was freed by Prophet Muhammad ﷺ, who called him “our brother and our freedman” and appointed him to command. He later appointed Zayd’s son Usama. When people objected to Usama’s leadership, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ defended both father and son as fully worthy of command.

   > "If you criticize his leadership, you used to criticize his father's leadership before. By God, he was worthy of leadership." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 3729-3730[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}; *Sahih al-Bukhari* 4469[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Social responsibility was expressed in practical acts.** Care for the vulnerable was not left as a sentiment. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ told his followers plainly: free the captive, feed the hungry, and visit the sick.

   > "Free the captive, feed the hungry, and visit the sick." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 3046[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Lineage could not compensate for a failure of character and action.** In a strongly tribal society, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ closed a teaching about mercy, mutual aid, and knowledge with a deliberately leveling principle.

   > "Whoever is slowed by his deeds will not be accelerated by his lineage." — *Sahih Muslim* 2699a[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Leniency toward debtors became a reason to hope for God’s own mercy.** Prophet Muhammad ﷺ told of a creditor whom God forgave because he had been gentle with people in financial difficulty.

   > "Give time to one in ease, and overlook one in hardship." Then God overlooked him. — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 2077[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Providing for one’s own family counts as charity when done sincerely.** Public generosity was not meant to come at the expense of obligations at home.

   > "When a Muslim spends on his family seeking reward, it is charity for him." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 5351[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **He preferred that a person leave heirs financially secure rather than deliberately dependent on others.** Poverty was not romanticized for its own sake.

   > "To leave your heirs well provided for is better than leaving them poor, begging from people." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 5354[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Wrongfully striking an enslaved person could require setting that person free.** Although slavery still existed, legal authority over another human being did not create an unrestricted right to violence.

   > "Whoever slaps or beats his slave, the expiation for it is to set him free." — *Sahih Muslim* 1657b[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Selling a free human being into slavery was condemned in the strongest terms.** In a hadith in which Prophet Muhammad ﷺ reports words from God, this is one of the abuses for which God Himself will be the offender’s adversary on the Day of Judgment.

   > "I will be the opponent of... a man who sells a free person and consumes the price." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 2270[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **People placed low in the old social hierarchy could receive the highest religious honor.** Bilal, a formerly enslaved Abyssinian Muslim, became the best-known caller to prayer in Prophet Muhammad’s ﷺ community. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ also told Bilal that he had heard his footsteps ahead of him in Paradise.

   > "Tell me of the deed you have done in Islam for which you most hope, for I heard your footsteps before me in Paradise." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 1149[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Poverty could never make a child’s life expendable.** The Qur’an expressly prohibited killing children out of fear over provision.

   > "Say, ˹O Prophet,˺ 'Come! Let me recite to you what your Lord has forbidden to you: do not associate others with Him ˹in worship˺. ˹Do not fail to˺ honour your parents. Do not kill your children for fear of poverty. We provide for you and for them. Do not come near indecencies, openly or secretly. Do not take a ˹human˺ life—made sacred by Allah—except with ˹legal˺ right. This is what He has commanded you, so perhaps you will understand.'" (**Sūrat al-Anʿām 6:151**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿۞ قُلْ تَعَالَوْا۟ أَتْلُ مَا حَرَّمَ رَبُّكُمْ عَلَيْكُمْ ۖ أَلَّا تُشْرِكُوا۟ بِهِۦ شَيْـًٔا ۖ وَبِٱلْوَٰلِدَيْنِ إِحْسَـٰنًا ۖ وَلَا تَقْتُلُوٓا۟ أَوْلَـٰدَكُم مِّنْ إِمْلَـٰقٍ ۖ نَّحْنُ نَرْزُقُكُمْ وَإِيَّاهُمْ ۖ وَلَا تَقْرَبُوا۟ ٱلْفَوَٰحِشَ مَا ظَهَرَ مِنْهَا وَمَا بَطَنَ ۖ وَلَا تَقْتُلُوا۟ ٱلنَّفْسَ ٱلَّتِى حَرَّمَ ٱللَّهُ إِلَّا بِٱلْحَقِّ ۚ ذَٰلِكُمْ وَصَّىٰكُم بِهِۦ لَعَلَّكُمْ تَعْقِلُونَ ۝١٥١﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الأَنۡعَامِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **An orphan’s property had to be protected as carefully as the orphan’s physical needs.** The Qur’an treats adults consuming an orphan’s wealth unjustly as a grave moral offense.

   > "Indeed, those who unjustly consume orphans' wealth ˹in fact˺ consume nothing but fire into their bellies. And they will be burned in a blazing Hell." (**Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:10**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿إِنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ يَأْكُلُونَ أَمْوَٰلَ ٱلْيَتَـٰمَىٰ ظُلْمًا إِنَّمَا يَأْكُلُونَ فِى بُطُونِهِمْ نَارًا ۖ وَسَيَصْلَوْنَ سَعِيرًا ۝١٠﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ النِّسَاءِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Charity could not be used to humiliate its recipient.** The Qur’an says that a kind word and forgiveness are better than a gift followed by hurt or reminders of the giver’s generosity.

   > "Kind words and forgiveness are better than charity followed by injury. And Allah is Self-Sufficient, Most Forbearing." (**Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:263**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿۞ قَوْلٌ مَّعْرُوفٌ وَمَغْفِرَةٌ خَيْرٌ مِّن صَدَقَةٍ يَتْبَعُهَآ أَذًى ۗ وَٱللَّهُ غَنِىٌّ حَلِيمٌ ۝٢٦٣﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ البَقَرَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **A person without money could still contribute.** When his companions asked what someone with no material means could give, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ named working, helping a person in distress, encouraging good, and finally simply refraining from harming others.

   > "Let him help one in desperate need... and if he does not, let him refrain from evil; that is charity for him." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 6022[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **He challenged the assumption that social success made a person superior to the poor.** When Sa‘d ibn Abi Waqqas thought his status distinguished him from those beneath him, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ redirected his attention to the weak members of the community.

   > "Are you given victory and provision except through the weak among you?" — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 2896[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Mercy toward children was joined to respect for older people.** One age group was not to be honored by neglecting another.

   > "He is not of us who does not show mercy to our young and recognize the right of our elders." — *Jami at-Tirmidhi* 1920[5](#ref-5){: .footnote}[16](#ref-16){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **He praised charity given in private.** Public giving can encourage others, but concealed giving can better protect both the giver’s sincerity and the recipient’s dignity.

   > "To give charity publicly is good, but to give to the poor privately is better for you, and will absolve you of your sins. And Allah is All-Aware of what you do." (**Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:271**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿إِن تُبْدُوا۟ ٱلصَّدَقَـٰتِ فَنِعِمَّا هِىَ ۖ وَإِن تُخْفُوهَا وَتُؤْتُوهَا ٱلْفُقَرَآءَ فَهُوَ خَيْرٌ لَّكُمْ ۚ وَيُكَفِّرُ عَنكُم مِّن سَيِّـَٔاتِكُمْ ۗ وَٱللَّهُ بِمَا تَعْمَلُونَ خَبِيرٌ ۝٢٧١﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ البَقَرَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **The Qur’an created a contractual route out of slavery and told owners to help finance it.** Slavery was not abolished in a single decree, but the law did more than regulate bondage: it also established routes by which enslaved people could become free.[54](#ref-54){: .footnote}[56](#ref-56){: .footnote}

   > "And let those who do not have the means to marry keep themselves chaste until Allah enriches them out of His bounty. And if any of those ˹bondspeople˺ in your possession desires a deed of emancipation, make it possible for them, if you find goodness in them. And give them some of Allah's wealth which He has granted you. Do not force your ˹slave˺ girls into prostitution for your own worldly gains while they wish to remain chaste. And if someone coerces them, then after such a coercion Allah is certainly All-Forgiving, Most Merciful ˹to them˺." (**Sūrat al-Nūr 24:33**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَلْيَسْتَعْفِفِ ٱلَّذِينَ لَا يَجِدُونَ نِكَاحًا حَتَّىٰ يُغْنِيَهُمُ ٱللَّهُ مِن فَضْلِهِۦ ۗ وَٱلَّذِينَ يَبْتَغُونَ ٱلْكِتَـٰبَ مِمَّا مَلَكَتْ أَيْمَـٰنُكُمْ فَكَاتِبُوهُمْ إِنْ عَلِمْتُمْ فِيهِمْ خَيْرًا ۖ وَءَاتُوهُم مِّن مَّالِ ٱللَّهِ ٱلَّذِىٓ ءَاتَىٰكُمْ ۚ وَلَا تُكْرِهُوا۟ فَتَيَـٰتِكُمْ عَلَى ٱلْبِغَآءِ إِنْ أَرَدْنَ تَحَصُّنًا لِّتَبْتَغُوا۟ عَرَضَ ٱلْحَيَوٰةِ ٱلدُّنْيَا ۚ وَمَن يُكْرِههُّنَّ فَإِنَّ ٱللَّهَ مِنۢ بَعْدِ إِكْرَٰهِهِنَّ غَفُورٌ رَّحِيمٌ ۝٣٣﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ النُّورِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **The same verse forbade forcing enslaved women into prostitution for profit.** Legal power over another person did not make sexual coercion for commercial gain acceptable.

   > "And let those who do not have the means to marry keep themselves chaste until Allah enriches them out of His bounty. And if any of those ˹bondspeople˺ in your possession desires a deed of emancipation, make it possible for them, if you find goodness in them. And give them some of Allah's wealth which He has granted you. Do not force your ˹slave˺ girls into prostitution for your own worldly gains while they wish to remain chaste. And if someone coerces them, then after such a coercion Allah is certainly All-Forgiving, Most Merciful ˹to them˺." (**Sūrat al-Nūr 24:33**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَلْيَسْتَعْفِفِ ٱلَّذِينَ لَا يَجِدُونَ نِكَاحًا حَتَّىٰ يُغْنِيَهُمُ ٱللَّهُ مِن فَضْلِهِۦ ۗ وَٱلَّذِينَ يَبْتَغُونَ ٱلْكِتَـٰبَ مِمَّا مَلَكَتْ أَيْمَـٰنُكُمْ فَكَاتِبُوهُمْ إِنْ عَلِمْتُمْ فِيهِمْ خَيْرًا ۖ وَءَاتُوهُم مِّن مَّالِ ٱللَّهِ ٱلَّذِىٓ ءَاتَىٰكُمْ ۚ وَلَا تُكْرِهُوا۟ فَتَيَـٰتِكُمْ عَلَى ٱلْبِغَآءِ إِنْ أَرَدْنَ تَحَصُّنًا لِّتَبْتَغُوا۟ عَرَضَ ٱلْحَيَوٰةِ ٱلدُّنْيَا ۚ وَمَن يُكْرِههُّنَّ فَإِنَّ ٱللَّهَ مِنۢ بَعْدِ إِكْرَٰهِهِنَّ غَفُورٌ رَّحِيمٌ ۝٣٣﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ النُّورِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Social duty is placed immediately beside the worship of God.** In a single verse, the Qur’an names parents, relatives, orphans, the poor, near and distant neighbors, companions, travelers, and people under one’s authority within the same moral obligation.

   > "Worship Allah ˹alone˺ and associate none with Him. And be kind to parents, relatives, orphans, the poor, near and distant neighbours, close friends, ˹needy˺ travellers, and those ˹bondspeople˺ in your possession. Surely Allah does not like whoever is arrogant, boastful." (**Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:36**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿۞ وَٱعْبُدُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ وَلَا تُشْرِكُوا۟ بِهِۦ شَيْـًٔا ۖ وَبِٱلْوَٰلِدَيْنِ إِحْسَـٰنًا وَبِذِى ٱلْقُرْبَىٰ وَٱلْيَتَـٰمَىٰ وَٱلْمَسَـٰكِينِ وَٱلْجَارِ ذِى ٱلْقُرْبَىٰ وَٱلْجَارِ ٱلْجُنُبِ وَٱلصَّاحِبِ بِٱلْجَنۢبِ وَٱبْنِ ٱلسَّبِيلِ وَمَا مَلَكَتْ أَيْمَـٰنُكُمْ ۗ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ لَا يُحِبُّ مَن كَانَ مُخْتَالًا فَخُورًا ۝٣٦﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ النِّسَاءِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

### B. Non-Muslims, religious difference, justice, peace, and war

1. **Hatred did not excuse injustice.** The Qur’an explicitly forbids hostility toward a people from distorting one’s judgment of what is just, placing a clear restraint on tribal and political bias.

   > "O believers! Stand firm for Allah and bear true testimony. Do not let the hatred of a people lead you to injustice. Be just! That is closer to righteousness. And be mindful of Allah. Surely Allah is All-Aware of what you do." (**Sūrat al-Māʾidah 5:8**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿يَـٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ كُونُوا۟ قَوَّٰمِينَ لِلَّهِ شُهَدَآءَ بِٱلْقِسْطِ ۖ وَلَا يَجْرِمَنَّكُمْ شَنَـَٔانُ قَوْمٍ عَلَىٰٓ أَلَّا تَعْدِلُوا۟ ۚ ٱعْدِلُوا۟ هُوَ أَقْرَبُ لِلتَّقْوَىٰ ۖ وَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ ۚ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ خَبِيرٌۢ بِمَا تَعْمَلُونَ ۝٨﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ المَائـِدَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Justice was required even against oneself or one’s own family.**

   > "O believers! Stand firm for justice as witnesses for Allah even if it is against yourselves, your parents, or close relatives. Be they rich or poor, Allah is best to ensure their interests. So do not let your desires cause you to deviate ˹from justice˺. If you distort the testimony or refuse to give it, then ˹know that˺ Allah is certainly All-Aware of what you do." (**Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:135**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿۞ يَـٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ كُونُوا۟ قَوَّٰمِينَ بِٱلْقِسْطِ شُهَدَآءَ لِلَّهِ وَلَوْ عَلَىٰٓ أَنفُسِكُمْ أَوِ ٱلْوَٰلِدَيْنِ وَٱلْأَقْرَبِينَ ۚ إِن يَكُنْ غَنِيًّا أَوْ فَقِيرًا فَٱللَّهُ أَوْلَىٰ بِهِمَا ۖ فَلَا تَتَّبِعُوا۟ ٱلْهَوَىٰٓ أَن تَعْدِلُوا۟ ۚ وَإِن تَلْوُۥٓا۟ أَوْ تُعْرِضُوا۟ فَإِنَّ ٱللَّهَ كَانَ بِمَا تَعْمَلُونَ خَبِيرًا ۝١٣٥﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ النِّسَاءِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **He rejected special treatment for the powerful.** When people sought leniency for a woman from an influential family in a theft case, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ said earlier communities had been ruined by punishing the weak while sparing the powerful. He then invoked his own daughter to make clear that his family was not exempt.

   > "By God, if Fatima daughter of Muhammad stole, I would apply the law to her." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 6788[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **The Qur’an summarizes public morality in terms of justice, excellence, and generosity, while explicitly forbidding oppression.**

   > "Indeed, Allah commands justice, grace, as well as courtesy to close relatives. He forbids indecency, wickedness, and aggression. He instructs you so perhaps you will be mindful." (**Sūrat al-Naḥl 16:90**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿۞ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ يَأْمُرُ بِٱلْعَدْلِ وَٱلْإِحْسَـٰنِ وَإِيتَآئِ ذِى ٱلْقُرْبَىٰ وَيَنْهَىٰ عَنِ ٱلْفَحْشَآءِ وَٱلْمُنكَرِ وَٱلْبَغْىِ ۚ يَعِظُكُمْ لَعَلَّكُمْ تَذَكَّرُونَ ۝٩٠﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ النَّحۡلِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Religious coercion was rejected as a matter of principle.** Faith cannot be made sincere by force.[57](#ref-57){: .footnote}[60](#ref-60){: .footnote}

   > "Let there be no compulsion in religion, for the truth stands out clearly from falsehood. So whoever renounces false gods and believes in Allah has certainly grasped the firmest, unfailing hand-hold. And Allah is All-Hearing, All-Knowing." (**Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:256**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿لَآ إِكْرَاهَ فِى ٱلدِّينِ ۖ قَد تَّبَيَّنَ ٱلرُّشْدُ مِنَ ٱلْغَىِّ ۚ فَمَن يَكْفُرْ بِٱلطَّـٰغُوتِ وَيُؤْمِنۢ بِٱللَّهِ فَقَدِ ٱسْتَمْسَكَ بِٱلْعُرْوَةِ ٱلْوُثْقَىٰ لَا ٱنفِصَامَ لَهَا ۗ وَٱللَّهُ سَمِيعٌ عَلِيمٌ ۝٢٥٦﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ البَقَرَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Non-Muslims who were not fighting the Muslim community were to be treated with kindness and justice.**

   > "Allah does not forbid you from dealing kindly and fairly with those who have neither fought nor driven you out of your homes. Surely Allah loves those who are fair." (**Sūrat al-Mumtaḥanah 60:8**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿لَّا يَنْهَىٰكُمُ ٱللَّهُ عَنِ ٱلَّذِينَ لَمْ يُقَـٰتِلُوكُمْ فِى ٱلدِّينِ وَلَمْ يُخْرِجُوكُم مِّن دِيَـٰرِكُمْ أَن تَبَرُّوهُمْ وَتُقْسِطُوٓا۟ إِلَيْهِمْ ۚ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ يُحِبُّ ٱلْمُقْسِطِينَ ۝٨﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ المُمۡتَحنَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Non-Muslims under covenant received serious religious protection.** Killing a protected non-Muslim was not treated as a lesser wrong because the victim followed another religion.[57](#ref-57){: .footnote}[60](#ref-60){: .footnote}

   > "Whoever kills a person under covenant will not smell the fragrance of Paradise." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 3166[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **He stood for the funeral procession of a Jewish man.** When someone pointed out that the deceased was Jewish, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ answered by appealing to the man’s common humanity.

   > He said, "Was it not a soul?" — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 1312[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **He personally visited a Jewish boy who had served him when the boy became ill.** Prophet Muhammad ﷺ invited him to Islam during the visit, but the report also shows that religious difference did not prevent personal care for someone who was sick.

   > "A Jewish boy used to serve the Prophet ﷺ, and he became ill, so the Prophet ﷺ went to visit him." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 1356[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Muslims were forbidden to insult what other people worshipped.** The Qur’an restrains even offensive speech that could provoke retaliatory blasphemy and deepen hostility.

   > "˹O believers!˺ Do not insult what they invoke besides Allah or they will insult Allah spitefully out of ignorance. This is how We have made each people's deeds appealing to them. Then to their Lord is their return, and He will inform them of what they used to do." (**Sūrat al-Anʿām 6:108**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَلَا تَسُبُّوا۟ ٱلَّذِينَ يَدْعُونَ مِن دُونِ ٱللَّهِ فَيَسُبُّوا۟ ٱللَّهَ عَدْوًۢا بِغَيْرِ عِلْمٍ ۗ كَذَٰلِكَ زَيَّنَّا لِكُلِّ أُمَّةٍ عَمَلَهُمْ ثُمَّ إِلَىٰ رَبِّهِم مَّرْجِعُهُمْ فَيُنَبِّئُهُم بِمَا كَانُوا۟ يَعْمَلُونَ ۝١٠٨﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الأَنۡعَامِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Dialogue with Jews and Christians was to be conducted in the best manner.**

   > "Do not argue with the People of the Book unless gracefully, except with those of them who act wrongfully. And say, 'We believe in what has been revealed to us and what was revealed to you. Our God and your God is ˹only˺ One. And to Him we ˹fully˺ submit.'" (**Sūrat al-ʿAnkabūt 29:46**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿۞ وَلَا تُجَـٰدِلُوٓا۟ أَهْلَ ٱلْكِتَـٰبِ إِلَّا بِٱلَّتِى هِىَ أَحْسَنُ إِلَّا ٱلَّذِينَ ظَلَمُوا۟ مِنْهُمْ ۖ وَقُولُوٓا۟ ءَامَنَّا بِٱلَّذِىٓ أُنزِلَ إِلَيْنَا وَأُنزِلَ إِلَيْكُمْ وَإِلَـٰهُنَا وَإِلَـٰهُكُمْ وَٰحِدٌ وَنَحْنُ لَهُۥ مُسْلِمُونَ ۝٤٦﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ العَنكَبُوتِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Religious invitation was to proceed through wisdom and good instruction, not abuse.**

   > "Invite ˹all˺ to the Way of your Lord with wisdom and kind advice, and only debate with them in the best manner. Surely your Lord ˹alone˺ knows best who has strayed from His Way and who is ˹rightly˺ guided." (**Sūrat al-Naḥl 16:125**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿ٱدْعُ إِلَىٰ سَبِيلِ رَبِّكَ بِٱلْحِكْمَةِ وَٱلْمَوْعِظَةِ ٱلْحَسَنَةِ ۖ وَجَـٰدِلْهُم بِٱلَّتِى هِىَ أَحْسَنُ ۚ إِنَّ رَبَّكَ هُوَ أَعْلَمُ بِمَن ضَلَّ عَن سَبِيلِهِۦ ۖ وَهُوَ أَعْلَمُ بِٱلْمُهْتَدِينَ ۝١٢٥﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ النَّحۡلِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **The Qur’an recognizes the value of protecting several kinds of houses of worship.** In explaining why aggression may be resisted, it names monasteries, churches, synagogues, and mosques as places in which God’s name is much mentioned.

   > "˹They are˺ those who have been expelled from their homes for no reason other than proclaiming: 'Our Lord is Allah.' Had Allah not repelled ˹the aggression of˺ some people by means of others, destruction would have surely claimed monasteries, churches, synagogues, and mosques in which Allah's Name is often mentioned. Allah will certainly help those who stand up for Him. Allah is truly All-Powerful, Almighty." (**Sūrat al-Ḥajj 22:40**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿ٱلَّذِينَ أُخْرِجُوا۟ مِن دِيَـٰرِهِم بِغَيْرِ حَقٍّ إِلَّآ أَن يَقُولُوا۟ رَبُّنَا ٱللَّهُ ۗ وَلَوْلَا دَفْعُ ٱللَّهِ ٱلنَّاسَ بَعْضَهُم بِبَعْضٍ لَّهُدِّمَتْ صَوَٰمِعُ وَبِيَعٌ وَصَلَوَٰتٌ وَمَسَـٰجِدُ يُذْكَرُ فِيهَا ٱسْمُ ٱللَّهِ كَثِيرًا ۗ وَلَيَنصُرَنَّ ٱللَّهُ مَن يَنصُرُهُۥٓ ۗ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ لَقَوِىٌّ عَزِيزٌ ۝٤٠﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الحَجِّ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Warfare was limited by an explicit prohibition on transgression.**[58](#ref-58){: .footnote}[59](#ref-59){: .footnote}

   > "Fight in the cause of Allah ˹only˺ against those who wage war against you, but do not exceed the limits. Allah does not like transgressors." (**Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:190**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَقَـٰتِلُوا۟ فِى سَبِيلِ ٱللَّهِ ٱلَّذِينَ يُقَـٰتِلُونَكُمْ وَلَا تَعْتَدُوٓا۟ ۚ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ لَا يُحِبُّ ٱلْمُعْتَدِينَ ۝١٩٠﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ البَقَرَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Women and children were not to be intentionally killed in war.** After a woman was found dead during a campaign, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ expressly forbade killing women and children.[58](#ref-58){: .footnote}[59](#ref-59){: .footnote}

   > "The Messenger of God ﷺ forbade the killing of women and children." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 3015[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **His instructions to military commanders prohibited both treachery and mutilation.**

   > "Do not embezzle, do not act treacherously, do not mutilate, and do not kill a child." — *Sahih Muslim* 1731a[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **When the enemy genuinely inclined toward peace, Muslims were told to do the same.**

   > "If the enemy is inclined towards peace, make peace with them. And put your trust in Allah. Indeed, He ˹alone˺ is the All-Hearing, All-Knowing." (**Sūrat al-Anfāl 8:61**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿۞ وَإِن جَنَحُوا۟ لِلسَّلْمِ فَٱجْنَحْ لَهَا وَتَوَكَّلْ عَلَى ٱللَّهِ ۚ إِنَّهُۥ هُوَ ٱلسَّمِيعُ ٱلْعَلِيمُ ۝٦١﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الأَنفَالِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Feeding a captive is explicitly praised in the Qur’an.** The captive is placed beside the poor and the orphan as someone whose need is to be met.

   > "and give food—despite their desire for it—to the poor, the orphan, and the captive." (**Sūrat al-Insān 76:8**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَيُطْعِمُونَ ٱلطَّعَامَ عَلَىٰ حُبِّهِۦ مِسْكِينًا وَيَتِيمًا وَأَسِيرًا ۝٨﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الإِنسَانِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Having the power to retaliate did not make full retaliation the highest moral choice.** The Qur’an repeatedly presents patience and forgiveness as better than taking everything one is legally entitled to take in return.

   > "And whoever is patient and forgives - indeed, that is of the matters [requiring] determination." (**Sūrat al-Shūrā 42:43**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَلَمَن صَبَرَ وَغَفَرَ إِنَّ ذَٰلِكَ لَمِنْ عَزْمِ ٱلْأُمُورِ ۝٤٣﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الشُّورَىٰ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Cooperation was to be built around what is good, never around wrongdoing or aggression.**

   > "O believers! Do not violate Allah's rituals ˹of pilgrimage˺, the sacred months, the sacrificial animals, the ˹offerings decorated with˺ garlands, nor those ˹pilgrims˺ on their way to the Sacred House seeking their Lord's bounty and pleasure. When pilgrimage has ended, you are allowed to hunt. Do not let the hatred of a people who once barred you from the Sacred Mosque provoke you to transgress. Cooperate with one another in goodness and righteousness, and do not cooperate in sin and transgression. And be mindful of Allah. Surely Allah is severe in punishment." (**Sūrat al-Māʾidah 5:2**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿يَـٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ لَا تُحِلُّوا۟ شَعَـٰٓئِرَ ٱللَّهِ وَلَا ٱلشَّهْرَ ٱلْحَرَامَ وَلَا ٱلْهَدْىَ وَلَا ٱلْقَلَـٰٓئِدَ وَلَآ ءَآمِّينَ ٱلْبَيْتَ ٱلْحَرَامَ يَبْتَغُونَ فَضْلًا مِّن رَّبِّهِمْ وَرِضْوَٰنًا ۚ وَإِذَا حَلَلْتُمْ فَٱصْطَادُوا۟ ۚ وَلَا يَجْرِمَنَّكُمْ شَنَـَٔانُ قَوْمٍ أَن صَدُّوكُمْ عَنِ ٱلْمَسْجِدِ ٱلْحَرَامِ أَن تَعْتَدُوا۟ ۘ وَتَعَاوَنُوا۟ عَلَى ٱلْبِرِّ وَٱلتَّقْوَىٰ ۖ وَلَا تَعَاوَنُوا۟ عَلَى ٱلْإِثْمِ وَٱلْعُدْوَٰنِ ۚ وَٱتَّقُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ ۖ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ شَدِيدُ ٱلْعِقَابِ ۝٢﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ المَائـِدَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Religious difference did not cancel the duty to maintain family ties.** Asma bint Abu Bakr asked whether she should receive and care for her polytheist mother. Prophet Muhammad ﷺ told her to preserve the relationship rather than use religious difference as a reason to sever it.

   > He said, "Yes. Maintain good relations with your mother." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 2620[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **He warned his own representative to fear the prayer of anyone he might oppress.** Before sending Muadh to Yemen, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ reminded him that public authority carries spiritual danger because the supplication of the wronged person has no barrier before God.

   > "Beware the supplication of the oppressed, for there is no barrier between it and God." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 2448[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **The prohibition of oppression was made universal.** In a hadith in which Prophet Muhammad ﷺ reports words from God, oppression is forbidden among people as such, not merely within a particular tribe, court, or political dispute.

   > "My servants, I have forbidden oppression for Myself and made it forbidden among you, so do not oppress one another." — *Sahih Muslim* 2577a[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **He openly acknowledged the limits of human judgment, including his own judgment of a case.** One litigant might present an argument more persuasively than another. A favorable ruling therefore could not make someone else’s property morally lawful to take.

   > "I am only human... perhaps one of you is more eloquent in presenting his case. If I award him his brother's right, let him not take it." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 7169[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **A judge was not to decide a case while angry.** The exercise of legal power required enough emotional self-control to keep rage from shaping a judgment.

   > "No judge should judge between two people while he is angry." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 7158[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Even an enemy polytheist who asked for protection had to be escorted to safety.** The Qur’an allowed the person to hear the Islamic message but still required safe passage afterward.

   > "And if anyone from the polytheists asks for your protection ˹O Prophet˺, grant it to them so they may hear the Word of Allah, then escort them to a place of safety, for they are a people who have no knowledge." (**Sūrat al-Tawbah 9:6**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَإِنْ أَحَدٌ مِّنَ ٱلْمُشْرِكِينَ ٱسْتَجَارَكَ فَأَجِرْهُ حَتَّىٰ يَسْمَعَ كَلَـٰمَ ٱللَّهِ ثُمَّ أَبْلِغْهُ مَأْمَنَهُۥ ۚ ذَٰلِكَ بِأَنَّهُمْ قَوْمٌ لَّا يَعْلَمُونَ ۝٦﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ التَّوۡبَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **He insisted on gentleness even in response to a deliberate insult.** When Jewish visitors used a hostile pun in greeting him and Aisha responded angrily, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ restrained her and appealed to the general value of gentleness.

   > "Be calm, Aisha. God loves gentleness in all matters." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 6024[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **The Qur’an rejects inherited or collective moral guilt.** No one bears another person’s sin merely because of family, tribe, religion, or group identity.

   > "Say, ˹O Prophet,˺ 'Should I seek a lord other than Allah while He is the Lord of everything?' No one will reap except what they sow. No soul burdened with sin will bear the burden of another. Then to your Lord is your return, and He will inform you of your differences." (**Sūrat al-Anʿām 6:164**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿قُلْ أَغَيْرَ ٱللَّهِ أَبْغِى رَبًّا وَهُوَ رَبُّ كُلِّ شَىْءٍ ۚ وَلَا تَكْسِبُ كُلُّ نَفْسٍ إِلَّا عَلَيْهَا ۚ وَلَا تَزِرُ وَازِرَةٌ وِزْرَ أُخْرَىٰ ۚ ثُمَّ إِلَىٰ رَبِّكُم مَّرْجِعُكُمْ فَيُنَبِّئُكُم بِمَا كُنتُمْ فِيهِ تَخْتَلِفُونَ ۝١٦٤﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الأَنۡعَامِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Prophet Muhammad ﷺ was not authorized to force belief, even when people’s unbelief grieved him.** The Qur’an repeatedly distinguishes his duty to convey the message from God’s authority over human hearts.

   > "Had your Lord so willed ˹O Prophet˺, all ˹people˺ on earth would have certainly believed, every single one of them! Would you then force people to become believers?" (**Sūrat Yūnus 10:99**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَلَوْ شَآءَ رَبُّكَ لَـَٔامَنَ مَن فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ كُلُّهُمْ جَمِيعًا ۚ أَفَأَنتَ تُكْرِهُ ٱلنَّاسَ حَتَّىٰ يَكُونُوا۟ مُؤْمِنِينَ ۝٩٩﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ يُونُسَ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **In the traditional Muslim reading, the Qur’an’s first permission to fight is framed as a response to persecution.** It speaks of people who had been wronged and driven from their homes, rather than presenting warfare as an end in itself.[58](#ref-58){: .footnote}[59](#ref-59){: .footnote}

   > "Permission ˹to fight back˺ is ˹hereby˺ granted to those being fought, for they have been wronged. And Allah is truly Most Capable of helping them ˹prevail˺. ˹They are˺ those who have been expelled from their homes for no reason other than proclaiming: 'Our Lord is Allah.' Had Allah not repelled ˹the aggression of˺ some people by means of others, destruction would have surely claimed monasteries, churches, synagogues, and mosques in which Allah's Name is often mentioned. Allah will certainly help those who stand up for Him. Allah is truly All-Powerful, Almighty." (**Sūrat al-Ḥajj 22:39-40**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿أُذِنَ لِلَّذِينَ يُقَـٰتَلُونَ بِأَنَّهُمْ ظُلِمُوا۟ ۚ وَإِنَّ ٱللَّهَ عَلَىٰ نَصْرِهِمْ لَقَدِيرٌ ۝٣٩ ٱلَّذِينَ أُخْرِجُوا۟ مِن دِيَـٰرِهِم بِغَيْرِ حَقٍّ إِلَّآ أَن يَقُولُوا۟ رَبُّنَا ٱللَّهُ ۗ وَلَوْلَا دَفْعُ ٱللَّهِ ٱلنَّاسَ بَعْضَهُم بِبَعْضٍ لَّهُدِّمَتْ صَوَٰمِعُ وَبِيَعٌ وَصَلَوَٰتٌ وَمَسَـٰجِدُ يُذْكَرُ فِيهَا ٱسْمُ ٱللَّهِ كَثِيرًا ۗ وَلَيَنصُرَنَّ ٱللَّهُ مَن يَنصُرُهُۥٓ ۗ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ لَقَوِىٌّ عَزِيزٌ ۝٤٠﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الحَجِّ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **He told soldiers not to wish for battle.** Courage when fighting became unavoidable was paired with a prior desire for peace and safety.

   > "Do not wish to meet the enemy; ask God for safety. But if you meet them, then be steadfast." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 3024-3025[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Once hostile people withdrew from fighting and offered peace, there was no basis to attack them.** The Qur’an kept armed action tied to an active state of hostility.

   > "except those who are allies of a people you are bound with in a treaty or those wholeheartedly opposed to fighting either you or their own people. If Allah had willed, He would have empowered them to fight you. So if they refrain from fighting you and offer you peace, then Allah does not permit you to harm them." (**Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:90**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿إِلَّا ٱلَّذِينَ يَصِلُونَ إِلَىٰ قَوْمٍۭ بَيْنَكُمْ وَبَيْنَهُم مِّيثَـٰقٌ أَوْ جَآءُوكُمْ حَصِرَتْ صُدُورُهُمْ أَن يُقَـٰتِلُوكُمْ أَوْ يُقَـٰتِلُوا۟ قَوْمَهُمْ ۚ وَلَوْ شَآءَ ٱللَّهُ لَسَلَّطَهُمْ عَلَيْكُمْ فَلَقَـٰتَلُوكُمْ ۚ فَإِنِ ٱعْتَزَلُوكُمْ فَلَمْ يُقَـٰتِلُوكُمْ وَأَلْقَوْا۟ إِلَيْكُمُ ٱلسَّلَمَ فَمَا جَعَلَ ٱللَّهُ لَكُمْ عَلَيْهِمْ سَبِيلًا ۝٩٠﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ النِّسَاءِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **The protection of noncombatants is made explicit in a report about a woman found slain.** Prophet Muhammad ﷺ observed that she had not been fighting and then ordered that women and hired servants not be killed.[58](#ref-58){: .footnote}[59](#ref-59){: .footnote}

   > "She was not one who would have fought." ... "Do not kill a woman or a hired servant." — *Sunan Abi Dawud* 2669[4](#ref-4){: .footnote}[15](#ref-15){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **He prohibited killing captives by burning.** After initially ordering that two enemies be burned if captured, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ reversed the command before it was carried out and said that punishment by fire belongs to God alone.

   > "If you capture them, kill them, but do not burn them, for no one should punish with fire except God." — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 3016[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **The Qur’an names gracious release and ransom as ways of dealing with prisoners after battlefield control has been established.** Capture was not presented as requiring permanent captivity.

   > "So when you meet the disbelievers ˹in battle˺, strike ˹their˺ necks until you have thoroughly subdued them, then bind them firmly. Later ˹free them either as˺ an act of grace or by ransom until the war comes to an end. So will it be. Had Allah willed, He ˹Himself˺ could have inflicted punishment on them. But He does ˹this only to˺ test some of you by means of others. And those who are martyred in the cause of Allah, He will never render their deeds void." (**Sūrat Muḥammad 47:4**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿فَإِذَا لَقِيتُمُ ٱلَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا۟ فَضَرْبَ ٱلرِّقَابِ حَتَّىٰٓ إِذَآ أَثْخَنتُمُوهُمْ فَشُدُّوا۟ ٱلْوَثَاقَ فَإِمَّا مَنًّۢا بَعْدُ وَإِمَّا فِدَآءً حَتَّىٰ تَضَعَ ٱلْحَرْبُ أَوْزَارَهَا ۚ ذَٰلِكَ وَلَوْ يَشَآءُ ٱللَّهُ لَٱنتَصَرَ مِنْهُمْ وَلَـٰكِن لِّيَبْلُوَا۟ بَعْضَكُم بِبَعْضٍ ۗ وَٱلَّذِينَ قُتِلُوا۟ فِى سَبِيلِ ٱللَّهِ فَلَن يُضِلَّ أَعْمَـٰلَهُمْ ۝٤﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ مُحَمَّدٍ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Even lawful retaliation had to remain proportionate, while patience was presented as better.** The injured party retained a right to respond without escalation being turned into a virtue.

   > "If you retaliate, then let it be equivalent to what you have suffered. But if you patiently endure, it is certainly best for those who are patient." (**Sūrat al-Naḥl 16:126**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَإِنْ عَاقَبْتُمْ فَعَاقِبُوا۟ بِمِثْلِ مَا عُوقِبْتُم بِهِۦ ۖ وَلَئِن صَبَرْتُمْ لَهُوَ خَيْرٌ لِّلصَّـٰبِرِينَ ۝١٢٦﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ النَّحۡلِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Treaties with religious opponents had to be honored as long as the other party honored them.** Religious disagreement did not make a solemn agreement disposable when breaking it became convenient.

   > "As for the polytheists who have honoured every term of their treaty with you and have not supported an enemy against you, honour your treaty with them until the end of its term. Surely Allah loves those who are mindful ˹of Him˺." (**Sūrat al-Tawbah 9:4**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿إِلَّا ٱلَّذِينَ عَـٰهَدتُّم مِّنَ ٱلْمُشْرِكِينَ ثُمَّ لَمْ يَنقُصُوكُمْ شَيْـًٔا وَلَمْ يُظَـٰهِرُوا۟ عَلَيْكُمْ أَحَدًا فَأَتِمُّوٓا۟ إِلَيْهِمْ عَهْدَهُمْ إِلَىٰ مُدَّتِهِمْ ۚ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ يُحِبُّ ٱلْمُتَّقِينَ ۝٤﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ التَّوۡبَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Keeping covenants was a religious duty, not merely a diplomatic tactic.** The Qur’an places the obligation before God, making fidelity morally binding even when opportunism would be easier.

   > "Honour Allah's covenant when you make a pledge, and do not break your oaths after confirming them, having made Allah your guarantor. Surely Allah knows all you do." (**Sūrat al-Naḥl 16:91**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَأَوْفُوا۟ بِعَهْدِ ٱللَّهِ إِذَا عَـٰهَدتُّمْ وَلَا تَنقُضُوا۟ ٱلْأَيْمَـٰنَ بَعْدَ تَوْكِيدِهَا وَقَدْ جَعَلْتُمُ ٱللَّهَ عَلَيْكُمْ كَفِيلًا ۚ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ يَعْلَمُ مَا تَفْعَلُونَ ۝٩١﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ النَّحۡلِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Justice could require a ruling against Muslims and in favor of a non-Muslim.** In the traditional account behind Qur’an 4:105–112, members of a Muslim clan stole property and tried to blame a Jewish man. The revealed passage rejected communal favoritism and warned Prophet Muhammad ﷺ not to defend the deceitful.

   > "Indeed, We have sent down the Book to you ˹O Prophet˺ in truth to judge between people by means of what Allah has shown you. So do not be an advocate for the deceitful." (**Sūrat al-Nisāʾ 4:105**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}; occasion report: *Jami at-Tirmidhi* 3036, graded *hasan* [good][5](#ref-5){: .footnote}[16](#ref-16){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿إِنَّآ أَنزَلْنَآ إِلَيْكَ ٱلْكِتَـٰبَ بِٱلْحَقِّ لِتَحْكُمَ بَيْنَ ٱلنَّاسِ بِمَآ أَرَىٰكَ ٱللَّهُ ۚ وَلَا تَكُن لِّلْخَآئِنِينَ خَصِيمًا ۝١٠٥﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ النِّسَاءِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Even an accusation that he had favored a relative was preserved in the record.** In an irrigation dispute involving his cousin al-Zubayr, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ first gave a conciliatory ruling that still allowed the other landowner to receive water. When the man angrily accused him of favoring his cousin, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ gave al-Zubayr his full legal entitlement. The report, including the accusation, was transmitted by al-Zubayr’s own son.

   > Muhammad ﷺ first told al-Zubayr to water and then release the water to his neighbor; after the objection, he ordered him to take his full right. — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 2359-2362[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **A captive mother was not to be separated from her child.** In a world where captives could be treated as property and families broken apart, the bond between parent and child was given a claim against the stronger party.

   > "Whoever separates a mother from her child, God will separate him from those he loves on the Day of Resurrection." — *Jami at-Tirmidhi* 1566, graded *hasan* [good][5](#ref-5){: .footnote}[16](#ref-16){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

### C. Money, work, knowledge, leadership, health, and the natural world

1. **Honest work carried its own dignity.** Prophet Muhammad ﷺ taught that the best food a person eats is earned through his own labor, and he cited the prophet David as an example.

   > “No one has eaten food better than that earned by the work of his own hands.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 2072[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Commercial honesty was tied directly to blessing.** Concealing defects and lying in a transaction could strip that blessing away.

   > “If the buyer and seller speak truthfully and disclose defects, their transaction is blessed; if they lie and conceal, its blessing is erased.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 2079[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Cheating people through weights and measures is explicitly condemned in the Qur’an.**

   > "Woe to the defrauders. Those who take full measure ˹when they buy˺ from people, but give less when they measure or weigh for buyers." (**Sūrat al-Muṭaffifīn 83:1-3**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَيْلٌ لِّلْمُطَفِّفِينَ ۝١ ٱلَّذِينَ إِذَا ٱكْتَالُوا۟ عَلَى ٱلنَّاسِ يَسْتَوْفُونَ ۝٢ وَإِذَا كَالُوهُمْ أَو وَّزَنُوهُمْ يُخْسِرُونَ ۝٣﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ المُطَفِّفِينَ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Withholding a worker’s earned wages was condemned in exceptionally severe terms.**

   > God says: “I will be the opponent on the Day of Resurrection of... a man who hires a worker, receives the full work from him, then does not pay his wage.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 2270[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **A wealthy debtor who deliberately delayed payment was committing an injustice.**

   > “Delay in payment by a wealthy person is injustice.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 2287[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Leniency in ordinary buying, selling, and collecting debts was treated as a virtue.**

   > “May God have mercy on a person who is easygoing when he sells, when he buys, and when he asks for what is owed.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 2076[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **The Qur’an forbids both wrongful consumption of wealth and bribery used to obtain what belongs to others.**

   > "Do not consume one another's wealth unjustly, nor deliberately bribe authorities in order to devour a portion of others' property, knowing that it is a sin." (**Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:188**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَلَا تَأْكُلُوٓا۟ أَمْوَٰلَكُم بَيْنَكُم بِٱلْبَـٰطِلِ وَتُدْلُوا۟ بِهَآ إِلَى ٱلْحُكَّامِ لِتَأْكُلُوا۟ فَرِيقًا مِّنْ أَمْوَٰلِ ٱلنَّاسِ بِٱلْإِثْمِ وَأَنتُمْ تَعْلَمُونَ ۝١٨٨﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ البَقَرَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Support for vulnerable groups was built into public finance.** Qur’an 9:60 assigns mandatory alms to the poor, the needy, administrators, people in bondage, debtors, travelers, and other specified public religious purposes.

   > "Alms-tax is only for the poor and the needy, for those employed to administer it, for those whose hearts are attracted ˹to the faith˺, for ˹freeing˺ slaves, for those in debt, for Allah's cause, and for ˹needy˺ travellers. ˹This is˺ an obligation from Allah. And Allah is All-Knowing, All-Wise." (**Sūrat al-Tawbah 9:60**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿۞ إِنَّمَا ٱلصَّدَقَـٰتُ لِلْفُقَرَآءِ وَٱلْمَسَـٰكِينِ وَٱلْعَـٰمِلِينَ عَلَيْهَا وَٱلْمُؤَلَّفَةِ قُلُوبُهُمْ وَفِى ٱلرِّقَابِ وَٱلْغَـٰرِمِينَ وَفِى سَبِيلِ ٱللَّهِ وَٱبْنِ ٱلسَّبِيلِ ۖ فَرِيضَةً مِّنَ ٱللَّهِ ۗ وَٱللَّهُ عَلِيمٌ حَكِيمٌ ۝٦٠﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ التَّوۡبَةِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **The pursuit of knowledge was itself a religious good.** The Qur’an even teaches Prophet Muhammad ﷺ to pray for an increase in knowledge.

   > "So high [above all] is Allah, the Sovereign, the Truth. And, [O Muhammad], do not hasten with [recitation of] the Qur'an before its revelation is completed to you, and say, 'My Lord, increase me in knowledge.'" (**Sūrat Ṭāhā 20:114**), Saheeh International[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿فَتَعَـٰلَى ٱللَّهُ ٱلْمَلِكُ ٱلْحَقُّ ۗ وَلَا تَعْجَلْ بِٱلْقُرْءَانِ مِن قَبْلِ أَن يُقْضَىٰٓ إِلَيْكَ وَحْيُهُۥ ۖ وَقُل رَّبِّ زِدْنِى عِلْمًا ۝١١٤﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ طه</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Knowledge appears at the very beginning of the message.** The first revealed command is to read or recite in God’s name, and the opening revelation immediately speaks of teaching and the pen.

   > "Read, ˹O Prophet,˺ in the Name of your Lord Who created— created humans from a clinging clot. Read! And your Lord is the Most Generous, Who taught by the pen— taught humanity what they knew not." (**Sūrat al-ʿAlaq 96:1-5**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿ٱقْرَأْ بِٱسْمِ رَبِّكَ ٱلَّذِى خَلَقَ ۝١ خَلَقَ ٱلْإِنسَـٰنَ مِنْ عَلَقٍ ۝٢ ٱقْرَأْ وَرَبُّكَ ٱلْأَكْرَمُ ۝٣ ٱلَّذِى عَلَّمَ بِٱلْقَلَمِ ۝٤ عَلَّمَ ٱلْإِنسَـٰنَ مَا لَمْ يَعْلَمْ ۝٥﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ العَلَقِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Seeking knowledge was described as a path toward Paradise.**

   > “Whoever travels a path seeking knowledge, God makes easy for him a path to Paradise.” — *Sahih Muslim* 2699a[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Reflection on the natural world was encouraged as part of faith.** The Qur’an repeatedly directs attention to the heavens, the earth, and the patterns within creation.

   > "Indeed, in the creation of the heavens and the earth and the alternation of the day and night there are signs for people of reason." (**Sūrat Āli ʿImrān 3:190**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿إِنَّ فِى خَلْقِ ٱلسَّمَـٰوَٰتِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ وَٱخْتِلَـٰفِ ٱلَّيْلِ وَٱلنَّهَارِ لَـَٔايَـٰتٍ لِّأُو۟لِى ٱلْأَلْبَـٰبِ ۝١٩٠﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ آلِ عِمۡرَانَ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **He distinguished revelation from ordinary human judgment in technical matters.** After commenting on date-palm pollination and seeing that the harvest suffered, Prophet Muhammad ﷺ did not protect his prestige by pretending agricultural expertise.

   > “You know better about the technical affairs of your worldly life.” — *Sahih Muslim* 2363[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Collective affairs were to be conducted through consultation.**

   > "Who respond to their Lord, establish prayer, conduct their affairs by mutual consultation, and donate from what We have provided for them." (**Sūrat al-Shūrā 42:38**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿وَٱلَّذِينَ ٱسْتَجَابُوا۟ لِرَبِّهِمْ وَأَقَامُوا۟ ٱلصَّلَوٰةَ وَأَمْرُهُمْ شُورَىٰ بَيْنَهُمْ وَمِمَّا رَزَقْنَـٰهُمْ يُنفِقُونَ ۝٣٨﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الشُّورَىٰ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

1. **Prophet Muhammad ﷺ himself was commanded to consult his followers.** The command appears in a verse telling him to remain gentle, pardon them, and seek their counsel after Uhud, a painful military defeat outside Medina.

   > "It is out of Allah's mercy that you ˹O Prophet˺ have been lenient with them. Had you been cruel or hard-hearted, they would have certainly abandoned you. So pardon them, ask Allah's forgiveness for them, and consult with them in ˹conducting˺ matters. Once you make a decision, put your trust in Allah. Surely Allah loves those who trust in Him." (**Sūrat Āli ʿImrān 3:159**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿فَبِمَا رَحْمَةٍ مِّنَ ٱللَّهِ لِنتَ لَهُمْ ۖ وَلَوْ كُنتَ فَظًّا غَلِيظَ ٱلْقَلْبِ لَٱنفَضُّوا۟ مِنْ حَوْلِكَ ۖ فَٱعْفُ عَنْهُمْ وَٱسْتَغْفِرْ لَهُمْ وَشَاوِرْهُمْ فِى ٱلْأَمْرِ ۖ فَإِذَا عَزَمْتَ فَتَوَكَّلْ عَلَى ٱللَّهِ ۚ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ يُحِبُّ ٱلْمُتَوَكِّلِينَ ۝١٥٩﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ آلِ عِمۡرَانَ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

   The early biography also records him accepting ideas that were not his own, including Salman al-Farisi’s unfamiliar proposal to defend Medina with a trench. At Hudaybiyyah, the canonical hadith record likewise preserves him acting on Umm Salama’s practical advice when the camp reached an impasse. **Consultation was something he practiced as well as taught.**

   > Salman al-Farisi's trench proposal — *Sīra*: Ibn Hisham[8](#ref-8){: .footnote}[19](#ref-19){: .footnote}[28](#ref-28){: .footnote}[37](#ref-37){: .footnote}[38](#ref-38){: .footnote}; Umm Salama's advice at Hudaybiyyah — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 2731-2732[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Leadership meant responsibility for others, not ownership of them.**

   > “Each of you is a shepherd, and each of you is responsible for those under his care.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 7138[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **No ruler had an unlimited claim to obedience.** Political authority ended where disobedience to God began.

   > “Obedience is only in what is right.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 7257[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Food and consumption were governed by moderation rather than habitual excess.** The Qur’an gives the general rule, while a reported practical teaching advises leaving substantial room rather than routinely eating to complete fullness.

   > "O Children of Adam! Dress properly whenever you are at worship. Eat and drink, but do not waste. Surely He does not like the wasteful." (**Sūrat al-Aʿrāf 7:31**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
   > <div dir="rtl" lang="ar" class="quran-arabic">﴿۞ يَـٰبَنِىٓ ءَادَمَ خُذُوا۟ زِينَتَكُمْ عِندَ كُلِّ مَسْجِدٍ وَكُلُوا۟ وَٱشْرَبُوا۟ وَلَا تُسْرِفُوٓا۟ ۚ إِنَّهُۥ لَا يُحِبُّ ٱلْمُسْرِفِينَ ۝٣١﴾ <span class="ayah-ref">سُورَةُ الأَعۡرَافِ</span></div>
   {: .quote }

   > “If more is necessary, then a third for food, a third for drink, and a third for breath.” — *Jami at-Tirmidhi* 2380[5](#ref-5){: .footnote}[16](#ref-16){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Planting could become an ongoing act of charity.** The benefit counted even when the food was eaten by an animal rather than a person.[61](#ref-61){: .footnote}[62](#ref-62){: .footnote}

   > “No Muslim plants a tree or sows a crop from which a person, bird, or animal eats except that it is charity for him.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 2320[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Compassion extended even to an animal many people would avoid.** Prophet Muhammad ﷺ told of a man whom God forgave after he gave water to a desperately thirsty dog. When asked whether kindness to animals is rewarded, he answered that it is.[62](#ref-62){: .footnote}

   > “In every living creature there is a reward.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 2363[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Cruelty to animals could be a grave sin.** In one of Prophet Muhammad’s ﷺ teachings, a woman was condemned for confining a cat until it died without either feeding it or allowing it to find food for itself.[62](#ref-62){: .footnote}

   > “A woman was punished because of a cat she confined until it died.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 3318[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Even lawful slaughter had to minimize the animal’s suffering.**

   > “God has prescribed excellence in all things... when you slaughter, slaughter well. Let one of you sharpen his blade and spare the animal suffering.” — *Sahih Muslim* 1955a[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Living creatures could not be used as targets for sport.**

   > “The Messenger of God ﷺ cursed whoever takes a living creature as a target.” — *Sahih Muslim* 1957a[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Cleanliness was treated as part of spiritual life.**

   > “Purity is half of faith.” — *Sahih Muslim* 223[3](#ref-3){: .footnote}[14](#ref-14){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Illness was a reason to seek treatment, not an excuse for fatalistic passivity.**

   > “God has not sent down a disease except that He has also sent down its cure.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 5678[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **He gave a clear rule for containing plague.** People were not to enter a land where an outbreak was occurring, nor flee from a place once plague had broken out there. Whatever one thinks of premodern medicine more broadly, the rule plainly works against carrying an epidemic across boundaries.[67](#ref-67){: .footnote}

   > “If you hear of plague in a land, do not enter it; and if it occurs in a land while you are there, do not leave it.” — *Sahih al-Bukhari* 5728[2](#ref-2){: .footnote}[13](#ref-13){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Ownership of a working animal created responsibility for its welfare.** When Prophet Muhammad ﷺ saw a severely emaciated camel, he rebuked its neglect rather than treating the animal as disposable equipment.

   > “Fear God regarding these animals. Ride them while they are in good condition, and feed them while they are in good condition.” — *Sunan Abi Dawud* 2548, graded *sahih* [sound][4](#ref-4){: .footnote}[15](#ref-15){: .footnote}[43](#ref-43){: .footnote}
   {: .quote }

1. **Serious debts were to be documented.** The Qur’an’s longest verse instructs believers to write down deferred financial obligations and use witnesses. Religious life therefore included ordinary safeguards against dispute and exploitation.

   > "O believers! When you contract a loan for a fixed period of time, commit it to writing. Let the scribe maintain justice between the parties. The scribe should not refuse to write as Allah has taught them to write. They will write what the debtor dictates, bearing Allah in mind and not defrauding the debt. If the debtor is incompetent, weak, or unable to dictate, let their guardian dictate for them with justice. Call upon two of your men to witness. If two men cannot be found, then one man and two women of your choice will witness—so if one of the women forgets the other may remind her. The witnesses must not refuse when they are summoned. You must not be against writing ˹contracts˺ for a fixed period—whether the sum is small or great. This is more just ˹for you˺ in the sight of Allah, and more convenient to establish evidence and remove doubts. However, if you conduct an immediate transaction among yourselves, then there is no need for you to record it, but call upon witnesses when a deal is finalized. Let no harm come to the scribe or witnesses. If you do, then you have gravely exceeded ˹your limits˺. Be mindful of Allah, for Allah ˹is the One Who˺ teaches you. And Allah has ˹perfect˺ knowledge of all things." (**Sūrat al-Baqarah 2:282**), Dr. Mustafa Khattab, *The Clear Quran*[1](#ref-1){: .footnote}[11](#ref-11){: .footnote}[12](#ref-12){: .footnote}[36](#ref-36){: .footnote}
   >
